    "answer": "The provided chunks mostly relate to other concepts within Computer Vision, Machine Learning, and Python.\n\nFor addressing your question about attention in Transformers and a Python example:\n\n- **Attention Mechanism**: In Transformers, especially in models like BERT, GPT, etc., the attention mechanism is used for weighted averaging of embeddings over input sequences. It allows the model to focus on relevant parts of the sequence at each position during prediction.\n\n- **Example with PyTorch**:\n```python\nimport torch\nfrom transformers import BertModel\n\n# Load a pre-trained BERT model and tokenizer\nmodel = BertModel.from_pretrained('bert-base-uncased')\ntokenizer = BertTokenizer.from_pretrained('bert-base-uncased')\n\n# Input sentence for which we want to compute attention\ninput_sentence = \"Deep learning is really cool.\"\n\n# Tokenize the input sentence\ninputs = tokenizer(input_sentence, return_tensors='pt', truncation=True, padding=True)\n\n# Get model output and embeddings\noutputs = model(**inputs)\nlast_hidden_states = outputs.last_hidden_state  # The last hidden state\n\n# Compute attention weights\nattention_weights = model Attention(last_hidden_states)  # Assuming a multi-headed attention mechanism\n\nprint(\"Attention weights:\", attention_weights.shape)  # Shape: (1, number_of_heads, sequence_length, sequence_length)\n\n# Visualize some attention weights (e.g., first head and last two tokens in input)\nvisualization_attention = attention_weights[:, 0, -2:]  # Visualization for the last two tokens\n\nprint(\"Visualization of attention for last two tokens:\", visualization_attention.shape)  # Shape: (1, 2, 768)\n\n# Plotting or visualizing these weights would involve additional libraries such as matplotlib or seaborn\n```\n\n**Note:** The example assumes a BERT model and uses its built-in multi-head self-attention mechanism. Actual Transformers might use different mechanisms depending on their design.\n\nPlease let me know if you need more details or have specific requirements for your query!"
